## Researcher–agent collaboration

### 1. Researcher — initial_request

Please inspect the staged repository and relevant data files, especially `data/capital.csv` and any analysis scripts/notebooks that perform the 100-year-bin PCA. Report the available fields, any relevant PCA setup or existing outputs, and propose a concise analysis workflow to determine how symbolic-capital variables (house size, daggers, hatchets/swords) score on PC1 and distribute along PC2. Flag consequential assumptions or uncertainties, including PCA sign conventions, binning/standardization, and whether the question concerns loadings or plotted observations. Do not perform the full analysis or state findings yet.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** initial_inspection; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The brief identifies the three symbolic-capital variables, the capital dataset, and 100-year bins, but gives no PCA loadings, implementation details, or results. Initial inspection and proposed approach are requested before authorizing analysis.

**Open objectives:** Inspect repository/data and identify relevant PCA implementation and outputs; Clarify consequential analytical assumptions and propose approach; Determine PC1 values and PC2 distribution for symbolic-capital components in main analysis

**Agent reply (plan)**

I inspected the staged repository without running a PCA or determining any component findings. It contains `data/capital.csv`, `data/time_series_data.csv`, `data/pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv`, and `task.json`; there are no staged Python/R scripts or notebooks. `capital.csv` has 39 rows and 15 columns: `BCE`, `group`, `color`, and 12 measure columns (`ZAxtSchwert`, `ZDolch`, `Zhausgr`, `ZCU_AU`, `Zamber`, `ZMonument`, `ZHort`, `ZSichel`, `ZBeil`, `ZMW`, `ZKeform`, `Zkeverz`). The three named symbolic-capital fields correspond to hatchets/swords, daggers, and house size. Rows cover BCE 4300 through 500 in 100-year steps. Numeric data are represented with decimal commas in CSV and should be parsed explicitly; the supplied fields are documented as Z values. `group` and `color` are categorical plotting metadata per the supplied schema.

Proposed concise workflow after approval: parse decimal-comma numeric fields; exclude `BCE`, `group`, and `color`; define successive 100-year slices and document whether PCA is fit independently within each slice (one row per slice poses a key feasibility issue) or on pooled/rolling data; reproduce the intended feature scaling and PCA if repository/source context clarifies them; extract PC1 loadings for the three symbolic variables and PC2 scores for observations, reporting time-bin labels and dispersion/range. If the intended phrase “values on PC1” means variable loadings rather than observation scores, report loadings explicitly and distinguish them from biplot coordinates. Do not infer direction from sign alone because PCA eigenvector signs are arbitrary; compare relative magnitudes/signs within each fitted PCA, and state any orientation convention used.

Consequential uncertainties: no analysis code or existing PCA outputs are staged, so the specific setup cannot be reproduced from repository scripts. The phrase “100-year time slices” could mean PCA independently per bin, a PCA on all years subsequently plotted/grouped by bin, or rolling windows; truly per-bin PCA with this file’s single annual observation per 100-year step may have too few observations for conventional covariance PCA. Although features have Z-prefixed names, it is unclear whether to standardize again, and whether the symbols are plotted as variable loadings or time-bin observations. Please approve before I proceed with the analysis.

**Shared evidence:**

Shell inspection found only task.json and the three CSV files (no .py/.R/.ipynb). `capital.csv` pandas inspection: shape (39,15), BCE 4300..500 in 100-year increments, no missing cells; three named variables and other 9 feature columns are string-parsed decimal-comma measures. Header/first rows confirm field names and values. The user explicitly requested inspection and workflow proposal only; no PCA was performed.

**Assumptions:** Column descriptions in the provided task metadata accurately identify `ZAxtSchwert` as hatchets/swords, `ZDolch` as daggers, and `Zhausgr` as house size.; The 39 rows are intended as chronological observations, with one row at each 100-year BCE value.

**Uncertainties:** The question's reference to an existing 100-year-bin PCA may rely on unprovided study context, code, or outputs.; Unclear whether independent PCA per time slice, time-binned scores from a pooled PCA, or another binning strategy is intended.; Unclear whether 'values on PC1' requests variable loadings or observation scores; PC2 'distribution' might similarly refer to scores/positions rather than loadings.; Scaling and PCA implementation details are absent; the Z-prefixed data might or might not be standardized again.

### 2. Researcher — next_task

Thanks—this identifies the key ambiguity: the file has one row per 100-year interval, so an independent PCA within each interval is not viable. Proceed with the main analysis using a pooled PCA across the 39 chronological observations and the documented Z-valued capital measures, unless the repository contains an unreported alternative specification. Parse decimal commas, omit BCE and categorical plotting fields from PCA, and report for house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords their PC1 variable loadings and their PC2 observation-score distribution over time. Clearly distinguish loadings from scores, state the scaling and PC orientation convention, and describe the PC2 spread/time pattern only to the extent supported by outputs. If the original intended workflow cannot be established, label pooled PCA as an analytical assumption rather than a confirmed study method.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** approach_review; **approach approved:** True

**Basis:** The inspection reports 39 observations in 100-year chronological steps, no analysis scripts or existing PCA outputs, and uncertainty whether PC1 values mean loadings or scores. A within-slice PCA cannot be estimated meaningfully with one observation per slice; pooled PCA is a transparent workable choice for answering both variable PC1 and temporal PC2 distribution.

**Open objectives:** Compute and report data-grounded PCA results for the three symbolic-capital variables, including PC1 loadings and PC2 observation-score distribution across time; document methodological assumptions and sign orientation.

**Agent reply (result)**

**Analytical assumption, not confirmed study method:** no alternative PCA specification, script, or existing PCA output is staged. Following the approved workflow, I ran one pooled PCA across all 39 rows and the 12 documented Z-valued capital measures, leaving out BCE, `group`, and `color`. I parsed decimal commas and centered the measures without an additional unit-variance rescaling. Their sample standard deviations are nearly 1 for most features (0.987–1.001), although `ZSichel` is 0.538; this makes the no-rescaling choice consequential. PC1 and PC2 account for 31.84% and 20.42% of total variance, respectively.

Under a transparent sign convention—orient each component so its largest-absolute variable loading is positive—the **PC1 variable loadings** are positive and moderate for all three named symbolic-capital variables: house size (`Zhausgr`) **+0.228**, daggers (`ZDolch`) **+0.279**, and hatchets/swords (`ZAxtSchwert`) **+0.301**. These are feature loadings, not the scores of individual time observations. PC1 is not dominated by these variables: copper/gold (+0.399) and pottery decoration (+0.381) have larger positive loadings, while pottery form is negative (−0.355). Component signs are arbitrary, so reversing PC1 would reverse all its loadings and scores without changing the PCA.

The **PC2 observation scores** (not variable loadings) show substantial time variation. With the same orientation convention, the observed range is **−3.788 to +2.414** (sample SD 1.515). The three earliest rows, 4300–4100 BCE, all score −3.788; scores then rise sharply (0.528 at 4000 BCE), are mostly positive from 4000 to 2100 BCE with notable peaks at 3500–3200 BCE (2.040–2.414), fluctuate around zero through 1500 BCE, become positive at 1400–1100 BCE (0.484–1.784), and are consistently negative from 1000 to 500 BCE (−1.020 to −1.685). Thus the pooled PC2 positions are not a uniform spread: there is a strong low outlying early block, a generally positive middle stretch with peaks, and negative late scores, but not a simple monotonic trend. These describe each row's combined position across all 12 measures, not the isolated PC2 distribution of each named variable. The PC2 variable loadings for those fields are house size +0.364, daggers +0.120, and hatchets/swords +0.067.

I used a NumPy eigendecomposition of the sample covariance matrix (equivalent to PCA on centered features), orienting each eigenvector by its largest-magnitude loading. This is an analytical reconstruction from the supplied rows, not proof of the study's exact PCA setup or of a PCA re-fit independently in each 100-year bin. The supplied data have one observation at each 100-year step, so independent within-bin PCA would not be feasible from these rows alone. See the executed analysis script and captured output for all loadings and scores.

**Shared evidence:**

Executed `analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py` successfully against data/capital.csv. It reports 39 rows, 12 included fields, sample SDs and eigenanalysis. Explained variance ratio [0.318408, 0.204184]; PC1 target loadings: Zhausgr +0.228288, ZDolch +0.279339, ZAxtSchwert +0.301037; PC2 target loadings respectively +0.3644, +0.1195, +0.0666. PC2 scores from BCE 4300 to 500 range between min −3.7878 (4300, 4200, 4100) and max +2.4137 (3200), with output time-indexed scores. Script: analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py (44 lines), captured executed output: analysis/pooled_capital_pca_output.txt (93 lines).

**Assumptions:** The 12 numeric columns after BCE, group, and color represent the intended PCA measures.; A pooled PCA across 39 observations is the appropriate analysis given the approved request; it is not asserted to reproduce the source study.; Because the inputs are documented as Z values and most have sample SD near one, the analysis centers but does not standardize columns again.; “PC1 variable loadings” means eigenvector coefficients; “PC2 distribution over time” means observation scores. PC2 feature loadings are additionally provided to keep those interpretations distinct.

**Uncertainties:** No repository script, notebook, methods text, or existing PCA output establishes the exact original study workflow, scaling, or component orientation.; `ZSichel` has sample SD 0.538 while most other features are near 1; standardizing all features to unit variance could change the PCA.; Pooled PC2 score patterns summarize joint variation across 12 features and should not be read as an isolated distribution of house size, daggers, or hatchets/swords.

### 3. Researcher — conclude

Under the pooled-PCA analytical assumption, the three symbolic-capital variables have positive, moderate PC1 loadings: house size +0.228, daggers +0.279, and hatchets/swords +0.301. Their PC2 variable loadings are also positive (+0.364, +0.120, and +0.067, respectively). Separately, the pooled observation scores on PC2 range from −3.788 to +2.414: the earliest dates are strongly negative, scores are generally positive through much of the middle period with peaks around 3500–3200 BCE, and late dates (1000–500 BCE) are negative. These are loadings and pooled row scores, not separate per-variable distributions across PC2. The pooled PCA was reconstructed from the supplied 39 rows and is not confirmed as the original study's method; component signs are arbitrary.

**Decision:** stop

**Review phase:** result_review; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The reported analysis used all 39 rows and 12 Z-valued variables, centered but did not re-standardize, and distinguished feature loadings from observation scores. It supplies the requested PC1 characterization and PC2 positioning while explicitly noting the absent source-study specification and arbitrary sign orientation.

**Open objectives:** None

{

  "name": "execute_command",
  "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Write and execute pooled PCA analysis script in workspace\", \"command\": \"mkdir -p analysis && cat > analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py <<'PY'\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"Pooled PCA on 39 capital observations; see notes in output for assumptions.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport pandas as pd\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\nDATA = 'data/capital.csv'\\ndf = pd.read_csv(DATA, decimal=',')\\nfeatures = [c for c in df.columns if c not in ('BCE', 'group', 'color')]\\nX = df[features].astype(float).to_numpy()\\n# Dataset columns are documented as Z-values. Center but do not apply another\\n# unit-variance scaling; all variables are approximately SD 1 already.\\nXc = X - X.mean(axis=0)\\ncov = np.cov(Xc, rowvar=False, ddof=1)\\neigenvalues, eigenvectors = np.linalg.eigh(cov)\\norder = np.argsort(eigenvalues)[::-1]\\neigenvalues = eigenvalues[order]\\neigenvectors = eigenvectors[:, order]\\nloadings = pd.DataFrame(eigenvectors[:, :2], index=features, columns=['PC1', 'PC2'])\\nscores = Xc @ eigenvectors[:, :2]\\n# PCA signs are arbitrary. For each component orient its largest-magnitude\\n# loading positively and flip its associated scores together.\\nfor j in range(2):\\n    i = int(np.argmax(np.abs(loadings.iloc[:, j].to_numpy())))\\n    if loadings.iloc[i, j] < 0:\\n        loadings.iloc[:, j] *= -1\\n        scores[:, j] *= -1\\nprint(f'Rows={len(df)}; features={len(features)}; BCE={df.BCE.min()}..{df.BCE.max()}')\\nprint('Sample SDs:')\\nprint(X.std(axis=0, ddof=1).round(4))\\nprint('Explained variance PC1/PC2:', (eigenvalues[:2] / eigenvalues.sum()).round(6))\\nprint('Target PC1 loadings:')\\nprint(loadings.loc[['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert'], 'PC1'].round(6))\\nprint('Full loadings PC1/PC2:')\\nprint(loadings.round(4))\\nout = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': df.BCE, 'PC1_score': scores[:, 0], 'PC2_score': scores[:, 1]}).sort_values('BCE', ascending=False)\\nprint('PC2 observation scores, descending BCE:')\\nprint(out[['BCE', 'PC2_score']].round(4).to_string(index=False))\\nprint('PC2 summary:')\\nprint(out.PC2_score.describe().round(4))\\nprint('PC2 extrema:')\\nprint(out.loc[out.PC2_score.idxmin(), ['BCE', 'PC2_score']])\\nprint(out.loc[out.PC2_score.idxmax(), ['BCE', 'PC2_score']])\\nout['broad_bin_BCE'] = pd.cut(out.BCE, [0, 1000, 1500, 2000, 2500, 3000, 3500, 4000, 4500])\\nprint('Broad-bin descriptive PC2 summaries (descriptive only):')\\nprint(out.groupby('broad_bin_BCE', observed=False).PC2_score.agg(['count', 'mean', 'std', 'min', 'max']).round(3))\\nPY\\npython analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py | tee analysis/pooled_capital_pca_output.txt\\nwc -l analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py analysis/pooled_capital_pca_output.txt\", \"timeout_seconds\": 60}",

  "arguments": "{\"stage\": \"result\", \"summary\": \"Deliver pooled capital PCA results and reproducible script\", \"answer\": \"**Analytical assumption, not confirmed study method:** no alternative PCA specification, script, or existing PCA output is staged. Following the approved workflow, I ran one pooled PCA across all 39 rows and the 12 documented Z-valued capital measures, leaving out BCE, `group`, and `color`. I parsed decimal commas and centered the measures without an additional unit-variance rescaling. Their sample standard deviations are nearly 1 for most features (0.987\\u20131.001), although `ZSichel` is 0.538; this makes the no-rescaling choice consequential. PC1 and PC2 account for 31.84% and 20.42% of total variance, respectively.\\n\\nUnder a transparent sign convention\\u2014orient each component so its largest-absolute variable loading is positive\\u2014the **PC1 variable loadings** are positive and moderate for all three named symbolic-capital variables: house size (`Zhausgr`) **+0.228**, daggers (`ZDolch`) **+0.279**, and hatchets/swords (`ZAxtSchwert`) **+0.301**. These are feature loadings, not the scores of individual time observations. PC1 is not dominated by these variables: copper/gold (+0.399) and pottery decoration (+0.381) have larger positive loadings, while pottery form is negative (\\u22120.355). Component signs are arbitrary, so reversing PC1 would reverse all its loadings and scores without changing the PCA.\\n\\nThe **PC2 observation scores** (not variable loadings) show substantial time variation. With the same orientation convention, the observed range is **\\u22123.788 to +2.414** (sample SD 1.515). The three earliest rows, 4300\\u20134100 BCE, all score \\u22123.788; scores then rise sharply (0.528 at 4000 BCE), are mostly positive from 4000 to 2100 BCE with notable peaks at 3500\\u20133200 BCE (2.040\\u20132.414), fluctuate around zero through 1500 BCE, become positive at 1400\\u20131100 BCE (0.484\\u20131.784), and are consistently negative from 1000 to 500 BCE (\\u22121.020 to \\u22121.685). Thus the pooled PC2 positions are not a uniform spread: there is a strong low outlying early block, a generally positive middle stretch with peaks, and negative late scores, but not a simple monotonic trend. These describe each row's combined position across all 12 measures, not the isolated PC2 distribution of each named variable. The PC2 variable loadings for those fields are house size +0.364, daggers +0.120, and hatchets/swords +0.067.\\n\\nI used a NumPy eigendecomposition of the sample covariance matrix (equivalent to PCA on centered features), orienting each eigenvector by its largest-magnitude loading. This is an analytical reconstruction from the supplied rows, not proof of the study's exact PCA setup or of a PCA re-fit independently in each 100-year bin. The supplied data have one observation at each 100-year step, so independent within-bin PCA would not be feasible from these rows alone. See the executed analysis script and captured output for all loadings and scores.\", \"payload\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Under a pooled-PCA analytical assumption, house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords have positive, moderate PC1 feature loadings (+0.228, +0.279, and +0.301, respectively) under the stated arbitrary sign orientation. Their PC2 variable loadings are also positive (+0.364, +0.120, +0.067), but the PC2 observation-score distribution is a separate quantity: scores across the 39 dates range from \\u22123.788 to +2.414, with early very negative values, broadly positive middle-period positions including peaks around 3500\\u20133200 BCE, and mostly negative values at 1000\\u2013500 BCE. ### S5. Assistant

Under the pooled-PCA analytical assumption, the three symbolic-capital variables have positive, moderate PC1 loadings: house size +0.228, daggers +0.279, and hatchets/swords +0.301. Their PC2 variable loadings are also positive (+0.364, +0.120, and +0.067, respectively). Separately, the pooled observation scores on PC2 range from −3.788 to +2.414: the earliest dates are strongly negative, scores are generally positive through much of the middle period with peaks around 3500–3200 BCE, and late dates (1000–500 BCE) are negative. These are loadings and pooled row scores, not separate per-variable distributions across PC2. The pooled PCA was reconstructed from the supplied 39 rows and is not confirmed as the original study's method; component signs are arbitrary.

  "command": "mkdir -p analysis && cat > analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py <<'PY'\n\"\"\"Pooled PCA on 39 capital observations; see notes in output for assumptions.\"\"\"\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\n\nDATA = 'data/capital.csv'\ndf = pd.read_csv(DATA, decimal=',')\nfeatures = [c for c in df.columns if c not in ('BCE', 'group', 'color')]\nX = df[features].astype(float).to_numpy()\n# Dataset columns are documented as Z-values. Center but do not apply another\n# unit-variance scaling; all variables are approximately SD 1 already.\nXc = X - X.mean(axis=0)\ncov = np.cov(Xc, rowvar=False, ddof=1)\neigenvalues, eigenvectors = np.linalg.eigh(cov)\norder = np.argsort(eigenvalues)[::-1]\neigenvalues = eigenvalues[order]\neigenvectors = eigenvectors[:, order]\nloadings = pd.DataFrame(eigenvectors[:, :2], index=features, columns=['PC1', 'PC2'])\nscores = Xc @ eigenvectors[:, :2]\n# PCA signs are arbitrary. For each component orient its largest-magnitude\n# loading positively and flip its associated scores together.\nfor j in range(2):\n    i = int(np.argmax(np.abs(loadings.iloc[:, j].to_numpy())))\n    if loadings.iloc[i, j] < 0:\n        loadings.iloc[:, j] *= -1\n        scores[:, j] *= -1\nprint(f'Rows={len(df)}; features={len(features)}; BCE={df.BCE.min()}..{df.BCE.max()}')\nprint('Sample SDs:')\nprint(X.std(axis=0, ddof=1).round(4))\nprint('Explained variance PC1/PC2:', (eigenvalues[:2] / eigenvalues.sum()).round(6))\nprint('Target PC1 loadings:')\nprint(loadings.loc[['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert'], 'PC1'].round(6))\nprint('Full loadings PC1/PC2:')\nprint(loadings.round(4))\nout = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': df.BCE, 'PC1_score': scores[:, 0], 'PC2_score': scores[:, 1]}).sort_values('BCE', ascending=False)\nprint('PC2 observation scores, descending BCE:')\nprint(out[['BCE', 'PC2_score']].round(4).to_string(index=False))\nprint('PC2 summary:')\nprint(out.PC2_score.describe().round(4))\nprint('PC2 extrema:')\nprint(out.loc[out.PC2_score.idxmin(), ['BCE', 'PC2_score']])\nprint(out.loc[out.PC2_score.idxmax(), ['BCE', 'PC2_score']])\nout['broad_bin_BCE'] = pd.cut(out.BCE, [0, 1000, 1500, 2000, 2500, 3000, 3500, 4000, 4500])\nprint('Broad-bin descriptive PC2 summaries (descriptive only):')\nprint(out.groupby('broad_bin_BCE', observed=False).PC2_score.agg(['count', 'mean', 'std', 'min', 'max']).round(3))\nPY\npython analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py | tee analysis/pooled_capital_pca_output.txt\nwc -l analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py analysis/pooled_capital_pca_output.txt",
  "timeout_seconds": 60
}

Cultural Capital consists of Diversity of Pottery form, Diversity of Pottery Decoration. 4. Economic Capital consists of Depot, Sickle, Axes & Celts, Monument Size. 5. Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Each sample from the pollen record used in the principal component analysis is absolutely dated and therefore the openness score (PC 1.) can be plotted as a time series, expressing human induced land clearance. The 'Belau_PC1' of pollen data (pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv) has been assumed to reflect openness. Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. The original openness score (Belau_PC1), the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_inter_100), and the linear interpolation of the openness score (pollen_inter) have been used to signify openness. 6. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. The growth rate is defined as the percentage change of the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_grate_100). 7. import PCA\\np='data/capital.csv'\\ndf=pd.read_csv(p, decimal=',')\\nfeatures=[c for c in df.columns if c not in ('BCE','group','color')]\\nX=df[features].astype(float)\\nprint('Rows',len(df),'features',len(features),'BCE range',df.BCE.min(),df.BCE.max())\\nprint('Means/stds:')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame({'mean':X.mean(),'sd_sample':X.std(ddof=1),'min':X.min(),'max':X.max()}).round(4).to_string())\\n# Data are documented as svd_solver='full')\\nscores=pca.fit_transform(X)\\nloadings=pd.DataFrame(pca.components_.T,index=features,columns=['PC1','PC2'])\\nprint('\\\\nExplained variance ratio',pca.explained_variance_ratio_,'total',sum(pca.explained_variance_ratio_))\\n# Orient each component so largest absolute feature coefficient is positive (transparent arbitrary convention).\\nfor j in range(2):\\n    if loadings.iloc[:,j].abs().idxmax() and loadings.iloc[:,j].loc[loadings.iloc[:,j].abs().idxmax()] < 0:\\n        loadings.iloc[:,j]*=-1\\n        scores[:,j]*=-1\\nprint('\\\\nLoadings:')\\nprint(loadings.round(4).to_string())\\ntarget=['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']\\nprint('\\\\nTarget PC1 no additional unit-variance scaling.\\nXc=X-X.mean(0)\\ncov=np.cov(Xc,rowvar=False,ddof=1)\\nvals,vecs=np.linalg.eigh(cov)\\nix=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]; vals=vals[ix]; vecs=vecs[:,ix]\\nloadings=pd.DataFrame(vecs[:,:2],index=features,columns=['PC1','PC2'])\\nscores=Xc@vecs[:,:2]\\n# Independently orient components with the largest absolute loading positive.\\nfor j in range(2):\\n    i=np.argmax(np.abs(loadings.iloc[:,j].values))\\n    if loadings.iloc[i,j]<0:\\n        loadings.iloc[:,j]*=-1; no additional unit-variance scaling.\\nXc=X-X.mean(0)\\ncov=np.cov(Xc,rowvar=False,ddof=1)\\nvals,vecs=np.linalg.eigh(cov)\\nix=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]; vals=vals[ix]; vecs=vecs[:,ix]\\nloadings=pd.DataFrame(vecs[:,:2],index=features,columns=['PC1','PC2'])\\nscores=Xc@vecs[:,:2]\\n# Independently orient components with the largest absolute loading positive.\\nfor j in range(2):\\n    i=np.argmax(np.abs(loadings.iloc[:,j].values))\\n    if loadings.iloc[i,j]<0:\\n        loadings.iloc[:,j]*=-1; scores[:,j]*=-1\\nprint('\\\\nExplained variance ratio',np.round(vals[:2]/vals.sum(),6),'cumulative',round((vals[:2]/vals.sum()).sum(),6))\\nprint('\\\\nLoadings (unit eigenvectors):')\\nprint(loadings.round(4).to_string())\\ntarget=['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']\\nprint('\\\\nTarget PC1 loadings:')\\nprint(loadings.loc[target,'PC1'].round(6).to_string())\\nscore_df=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':df.BCE,'PC1_score':scores[:,0],'PC2_score':scores[:,1]}).sort_values('BCE',ascending=False)\\nprint('\\\\nPC2 scores in chronological descending BCE:')\\nprint(score_df[['BCE','PC2_score']].round(4).to_string(index=False))\\nprint('\\\\nPC2 summary',score_df.PC2_score.describe().round(4).to_dict())\\nprint('PC2 extrema')\\nprint('min',score_df.loc[score_df.PC2_score.idxmin(),['BCE','PC2_score']].to_dict())\\nprint('max',score_df.loc[score_df.PC2_score.idxmax(),['BCE','PC2_score']].to_dict())\\nscore_df['period']=pd.cut(score_df.BCE,bins=[0,1000,1500,2000,2500,3000,3500,4000,4500],right=True)\\nprint('\\\\nBroad-bin PC2 summaries')\\nprint(score_df.groupby('period',observed=False).PC2_score.agg(['count','mean','std','min','max']).round(3).to_string())\\nPY\\npython /tmp/pca_analysis.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 60}"

Center but do not apply another\\n# unit-variance scaling; all variables are approximately SD 1 already.\\nXc = X - X.mean(axis=0)\\ncov = np.cov(Xc, rowvar=False, ddof=1)\\neigenvalues, eigenvectors = np.linalg.eigh(cov)\\norder = np.argsort(eigenvalues)[::-1]\\neigenvalues = eigenvalues[order]\\neigenvectors = eigenvectors[:, order]\\nloadings = pd.DataFrame(eigenvectors[:, :2], index=features, columns=['PC1', 'PC2'])\\nscores = Xc @ eigenvectors[:, :2]\\n# PCA signs are arbitrary. For each component orient its largest-magnitude\\n# loading positively and flip its associated scores together.\\nfor j in range(2):\\n    i = int(np.argmax(np.abs(loadings.iloc[:, j].to_numpy())))\\n    if loadings.iloc[i, j] < 0:\\n        loadings.iloc[:, j] *= -1\\n        scores[:, j] *= -1\\nprint(f'Rows={len(df)}; features={len(features)}; BCE={df.BCE.min()}..{df.BCE.max()}')\\nprint('Sample SDs:')\\nprint(X.std(axis=0, ddof=1).round(4))\\nprint('Explained variance PC1/PC2:', (eigenvalues[:2] / eigenvalues.sum()).round(6))\\nprint('Target PC1 loadings:')\\nprint(loadings.loc[['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert'], 'PC1'].round(6))\\nprint('Full loadings PC1/PC2:')\\nprint(loadings.round(4))\\nout = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': df.BCE, 'PC1_score': scores[:, 0], 'PC2_score': scores[:, 1]}).sort_values('BCE', ascending=False)\\nprint('PC2 observation scores, descending BCE:')\\nprint(out[['BCE', 'PC2_score']].round(4).to_string(index=False))\\nprint('PC2 summary:')\\nprint(out.PC2_score.describe().round(4))\\nprint('PC2 extrema:')\\nprint(out.loc[out.PC2_score.idxmin(), ['BCE', 'PC2_score']])\\nprint(out.loc[out.PC2_score.idxmax(), ['BCE', 'PC2_score']])\\nout['broad_bin_BCE'] = pd.cut(out.BCE, [0, 1000, 1500, 2000, 2500, 3000, 3500, 4000, 4500])\\nprint('Broad-bin descriptive PC2 summaries (descriptive only):')\\nprint(out.groupby('broad_bin_BCE', observed=False).PC2_score.agg(['count', 'mean', 'std', 'min', 'max']).round(3))\\nPY\\npython analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py | tee analysis/pooled_capital_pca_output.txt\\nwc -l analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py analysis/pooled_capital_pca_output.txt\",\"timeout_seconds\":60}"

        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Write and execute pooled PCA analysis script in workspace\", \"command\": \"mkdir -p analysis && cat > analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py <<'PY'\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"Pooled PCA on 39 capital observations; see notes in output for assumptions.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport pandas as pd\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\nDATA = 'data/capital.csv'\\ndf = pd.read_csv(DATA, decimal=',')\\nfeatures = [c for c in df.columns if c not in ('BCE', 'group', 'color')]\\nX = df[features].astype(float).to_numpy()\\n# Dataset columns are documented as Z-values. For each component orient its largest-magnitude\\n# loading positively and flip its associated scores together.\\nfor j in range(2):\\n    i = int(np.argmax(np.abs(loadings.iloc[:, j].to_numpy())))\\n    if loadings.iloc[i, j] < 0:\\n        loadings.iloc[:, j] *= -1\\n        scores[:, j] *= -1\\nprint(f'Rows={len(df)}; features={len(features)}; BCE={df.BCE.min()}..{df.BCE.max()}')\\nprint('Sample SDs:')\\nprint(X.std(axis=0, ddof=1).round(4))\\nprint('Explained variance PC1/PC2:', (eigenvalues[:2] / eigenvalues.sum()).round(6))\\nprint('Target PC1 loadings:')\\nprint(loadings.loc[['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert'], 'PC1'].round(6))\\nprint('Full loadings PC1/PC2:')\\nprint(loadings.round(4))\\nout = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': df.BCE, 'PC1_score': scores[:, 0], 'PC2_score': scores[:, 1]}).sort_values('BCE', ascending=False)\\nprint('PC2 observation scores, descending BCE:')\\nprint(out[['BCE', 'PC2_score']].round(4).to_string(index=False))\\nprint('PC2 summary:')\\nprint(out.PC2_score.describe().round(4))\\nprint('PC2 extrema:')\\nprint(out.loc[out.PC2_score.idxmin(), ['BCE', 'PC2_score']])\\nprint(out.loc[out.PC2_score.idxmax(), ['BCE', 'PC2_score']])\\nout['broad_bin_BCE'] = pd.cut(out.BCE, [0, 1000, 1500, 2000, 2500, 3000, 3500, 4000, 4500])\\nprint('Broad-bin descriptive PC2 summaries (descriptive only):')\\nprint(out.groupby('broad_bin_BCE', observed=False).PC2_score.agg(['count', 'mean', 'std', 'min', 'max']).round(3))\\nPY\\npython analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py | tee analysis/pooled_capital_pca_output.txt\\nwc -l analysis/pooled_capital_pca.py analysis/pooled_capital_pca_output.txt\", \"timeout_seconds\": 60}"
